Like I can see the accident happening in my head if I don't touch the door handle the right way. \n\nWhich is weird because I've never actually been in an accident myself. Well, except for this one time when I was like ten, I was riding my bike down this massive hill near my house. It was one of those old Schwinns, you know, with the banana seat? My dad bought it at a garage sale for like five bucks. He was always doing that, dragging home junk from yard sales and trying to fix it up. He once brought home a broken lawnmower and spent three weeks taking the engine apart on the kitchen table. My mom was so mad, there was oil everywhere. She made him sleep on the couch for a week. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I was flying down this hill on that bike, and the chain just snapped. I went right over the handlebars and into Mrs. Gable's rose bushes. I had scratches all over my face for a month. Mrs. Gable was this really old lady who used to give out full-sized candy bars on Halloween. She was awesome. But yeah, other than that bike thing, I've never been in a real crash. But I still feel like I have to check everything, or else it's gonna happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there's something specifically wrong with the way you're doing the safety checks that you're trying to fix by repeating them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's... it's like I was saying before. It's not that I think I'm doing it wrong the first time, exactly. It's just that it doesn't *feel* right. Like, I'll do the whole loop—tires, lights, doors, the meter, the paperwork—and I'll even take the pictures on my phone. But if that feeling isn't perfect, I have to restart the whole thing. Even when I've got people standing right there on the curb waiting for me and I'm already super late. \n\nSpeaking of phones, I dropped mine the other day right in a puddle. It was raining so hard, like, sideways rain. I had to put it in a bag of rice for two days. Have you ever tried that? It actually works, which is crazy to me. My buddy Dave told me about it. Dave's the guy who got me the job driving the taxi, actually. We've known each other since middle school. We used to play in this terrible garage band together. He played drums, and I played bass, but I was awful at it. I could never keep the rhythm going. We only ever learned one song, and it was a blink-182 cover. We played it at the eighth-grade talent show and the microphone cut out halfway through. It was so embarrassing. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, if the check doesn't feel right, I just have to do it all over again until it does.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to perform the safety checks in a specific order or in a certain way to make it feel right?\n\nPatient: Yeah, there's definitely an order. I always start with the front left tire, then the lights, then the doors, check the meter, make sure the paperwork is in the glovebox... and then I take the pictures on my phone. If I mess up the order or if it just doesn't hit that perfect feeling, I have to go back to the front left tire and start the whole loop over. Even if the passengers are literally standing right there on the curb, getting mad because I'm already late. \n\nIt's stressful, you know? Being late all the time. I got this watch from my grandpa to help me keep track of time, but it's one of those old wind-up ones. It's got this really loud tick. Tick, tick, tick. Sometimes when I'm driving, it's all I can hear. My grandpa used to work at a clock factory, actually. He had hundreds of clocks in his house. Every hour, on the hour, his whole living room would just erupt in chimes and cuckoos and bells. It used to scare the life out of my dog, Buster. Buster was a golden retriever mix, sweetest dog ever, but he was terrified of loud noises. Fourth of July was a nightmare with him. We'd have to put him in the basement with the radio on full blast just so he wouldn't shake himself to pieces. \n\nBut yeah, I have to do the checks in that exact order, over and over, until it feels right before I can even think about taking a ride.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear of something bad happening, like a crash, the only thing that makes you feel like you need to check the car over and over?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, the crash is a huge part of it, but it's also... it's just this thought that keeps hitting me, you know? Like, even when I know I did the whole safety check, this voice in my head is like, \"You missed something on the cab, someone's gonna get hurt,\" or, \"Your boss is gonna think you're lying about doing the checks.\" And I just can't make it shut up. It's so loud in my head while I'm driving, or even when I'm just trying to study for my community college classes. \n\nI'm taking this intro to psychology class right now, actually. It's pretty tough. The professor is this guy who wears bowties every single day. Like, who wears a bowtie on a Tuesday morning? He told us he has a collection of over two hundred of them. Two hundred! I don't even own a regular tie. The last time I wore a tie was for my high school graduation, and it was a clip-on because I couldn't figure out how to tie a real one. My mom tried to show me from a YouTube video, but we both just ended up frustrated. We ended up getting pizza instead. It was from this place down the street called Mario's. They make the best garlic knots. They soak them in so much butter, it's probably terrible for you, but they're amazing. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, it's just this constant thought that I missed something and someone's gonna get hurt or I'll get in trouble, and it just won't stop playing in my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the safety checks need to be done in a way that feels symmetrical or just right, beyond just making sure everything is safe?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's totally about that \"just right\" feeling. I mean, I know the tires are fine, but I still have to keep going around the car—tires, lights, doors, the meter, the paperwork—over and over. And I take the pictures, too. But if it doesn't feel perfectly right, I just have to restart the whole check. Even with the passengers standing right there, staring at me while I'm already super late. \n\nSpeaking of being late, I actually got a parking ticket the other day because I was rushing so much. I parked too close to a fire hydrant. The ticket was like eighty bucks! I tried to appeal it online, but the city website is so confusing. It's like it was designed in the 90s. My friend Sarah works in IT, and she says those government websites are always a mess because they never want to pay for upgrades. She actually built a website for her cat. Like, a whole blog where she posts pictures of him sleeping in different boxes. His name is Mr. Whiskers, which is kind of basic, but he's a tuxedo cat, so it fits. He's really fat, too. She put him on a diet, but he just screams at her until she gives him treats. I don't know how she deals with it. I'm more of a dog person anyway, like I was saying about Buster. \n\nBut yeah, I just can't start the ride until I've done the whole loop and it finally feels right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the safety checks need to be done in a very specific or exact way, beyond just checking that everything is safe?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean, like I was just saying, it's exactly that. Before I can even take a ride, I keep going around the car checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, and the paperwork over and over again. And I take the pictures, and if it doesn't feel exactly right, I just restart the whole check. My mom keeps trying to throw it in the trash when he's not looking, but he literally hides it out in the garage behind his air compressor. I don't know if I'd call it balanced or symmetrical, exactly. It's more about just doing the whole loop until my brain finally stops screaming at me that it's wrong. So I guess not symmetrical, no. I just need it to feel right. \n\nSpeaking of feeling right, my steering wheel has been vibrating a little when I hit like, sixty on the highway. I think I might need an alignment. I took it to this mechanic down on 4th Street, this guy named Sal. Sal is a character, man. He's always smoking these cheap cigars that smell like burnt rubber, which I guess fits for a mechanic shop. He told me he's been working on cars since he was twelve. Twelve! When I was twelve, I was just playing Call of Duty in my basement and eating Doritos until my fingers were permanently orange. I used to play with this kid from Australia online. We'd stay up until like 4 AM my time just to do these zombie survival maps. His name was Liam, and he had this crazy thick accent. He tried to explain to me what Vegemite tastes like once, and honestly, it sounded disgusting. Like salty yeast paste? No thanks. I'll stick to peanut butter. Crunchy peanut butter, though. Creamy is just... it's just wrong. It sticks to the roof of your mouth way too much. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, it's not really about making it symmetrical. I got this really nice canvas one at a thrift store in Brooklyn for like ten bucks. It smells a little bit like mothballs, but it holds a ton of stuff. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========

Like, a whole blog where she posts pictures of him sleeping in different boxes. His name is Mr. Whiskers, which is kind of basic, but he's a tuxedo cat, so it fits. He's really fat, too. She put him on a diet, but he just screams at her until she gives him treats. I don't know how she deals with it. I'm more of a dog person anyway, like I was saying about Buster. \n\nBut yeah, I just can't start the ride until I've done the whole loop and it finally feels right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the safety checks need to be done in a very specific or exact way, beyond just checking that everything is safe?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean, like I was just saying, it's exactly that. Before I can even take a ride, I keep going around the car checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, and the paperwork over and over again. And I take the pictures, and if it doesn't feel exactly right, I just restart the whole check. It doesn't matter if the passengers are waiting right there on the sidewalk and I'm already super late. \n\nHonestly, taking all those pictures is totally killing my phone storage. I have like, thousands of photos of my taxi's tires and doors on my camera roll. I actually had to delete my Spotify app the other day just to make room for more of them, which really sucks because I love listening to music when I'm driving around empty. I've been super into 90s grunge lately. My dad actually saw Nirvana live back in the day. He still has the vintage tour shirt, but it's got all these moth holes in it now. My mom keeps trying to throw it in the trash when he's not looking, but he literally hides it out in the garage behind his air compressor. He's got this huge setup out there because he likes to fix up old dirt bikes on the weekends. He bought this vintage Honda a few months ago that didn't even run, and he spent... I don't know, maybe three weeks just taking the engine apart on his workbench. \n\nBut yeah, the checks have to be exact, or I just can't start the route.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the safety checks need to be done in a way that feels balanced or symmetrical to you?\n\nPatient: Um, balanced? I don't know if I'd call it balanced or symmetrical, exactly. It's more about just doing the whole loop until my brain finally stops screaming at me that it's wrong. So I guess not symmetrical, no. I just need it to feel right. \n\nSpeaking of feeling right, my steering wheel has been vibrating a little when I hit like, sixty on the highway. I think I might need an alignment. I took it to this mechanic down on 4th Street, this guy named Sal. Sal is a character, man. He's always smoking these cheap cigars that smell like burnt rubber, which I guess fits for a mechanic shop. He told me he's been working on cars since he was twelve. Twelve! When I was twelve, I was just playing Call of Duty in my basement and eating Doritos until my fingers were permanently orange. I used to play with this kid from Australia online. We'd stay up until like 4 AM my time just to do these zombie survival maps. His name was Liam, and he had this crazy thick accent. He tried to explain to me what Vegemite tastes like once, and honestly, it sounded disgusting. Like salty yeast paste? No thanks. I'll stick to peanut butter. Crunchy peanut butter, though. Creamy is just... it's just wrong. It sticks to the roof of your mouth way too much. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, it's not really about making it symmetrical. It's just the loop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like doing the safety checks over and over gives you a sense of control over what might happen on the road?\n\nPatient: Control? I don't know, I haven't really thought about it like that. I definitely don't feel in control of the road, no matter how many times I check my car. People out there drive like absolute maniacs anyway. Like just yesterday, this guy cut me off in a green Honda Civic, and it had this massive, ridiculous spoiler on the back. I don't get why people put spoilers on cheap front-wheel-drive cars. \n\nMy buddy Jake did that back in high school. He had this totally beat-up 2004 Corolla and spent his entire paycheck at AutoZone buying fake chrome trim and this exhaust that made it sound like a broken lawnmower. He worked at the movie theater, so his paycheck was basically nothing to begin with. But the perk was we used to get free popcorn from him. He'd literally bring home these giant, clear trash bags full of the leftover popcorn at the end of his shift. My mom absolutely hated it because it made our whole house smell like fake movie theater butter for days. She's super sensitive to smells, actually. Like, she can't even walk down the detergent aisle at the grocery store without getting a massive headache. She only buys this weird unscented eco-friendly soap now, which honestly sucks because it doesn't even get the grease stains out of my work shirts. \n\nSo, yeah, I don't really think doing the loop gives me any control over the road or accidents or whatever. I don't know what it does, I just have to do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the safety checks have to be done in a very precise or perfect way to make you feel okay about starting the ride?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. It has to be perfect. Like I was saying before, I can't even start a ride until I go around the car checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork... just over and over. And I have to take the pictures, and if the whole routine doesn't feel absolutely right, I just scrap it and start over from the beginning. It doesn't even matter if I'm already running super late and the passengers are literally standing right there on the curb staring at me. \n\nSpeaking of passengers staring, I had this one lady last week who just glared at me the entire ride. She was wearing this giant, floppy sun hat, which was weird because it was pouring rain. Like, absolute torrential downpour. The wipers were on full blast and they were still barely keeping up. I actually need to replace those wiper blades soon. They leave these annoying streaks right in my line of sight. I usually get the Michelin ones from Costco. Have you ever had the hot dogs at the Costco food court? They are so good, and it's still only like a buck fifty for a hot dog and a soda. My buddy Jake and I used to go there for lunch all the time in high school. We'd sneak out during third period study hall just to get cheap pizza and hot dogs. The security guard, Mr. Henderson, totally knew we were leaving, but he never said anything because Jake's older brother used to cut his grass for free or something like that. Mr. Henderson was a cool guy. He always wore these really bright, neon-colored running shoes with his uniform, which looked ridiculous but he said he had bad arches. \n\nBut yeah, if the checks aren't done perfectly and it doesn't feel right, I just have to restart the whole thing, no matter who's waiting.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to achieve a perfect state with your safety checks, or are you primarily trying to avoid something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I think it's... well, it's definitely about getting it perfect. I mean, I don't really think about avoiding a crash or anything like that while I'm doing it. It's just that feeling, you know? Like I said, before I can even take a ride, I'm just going around the car, checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork... just over and over. And taking the pictures. And if it doesn't feel right, I just have to restart the whole check. Even if I'm already late and the passengers are just standing there waiting for me. \n\nSpeaking of waiting, I had to wait in line at the DMV for like three hours last month just to renew my hack license. It was miserable. The AC was broken, and there was this guy sitting next to me who kept clipping his fingernails right there in the waiting area. Just *snip, snip, snip*. Who does that in public? It drove me absolutely insane. I tried to put my headphones in, but I forgot to charge them the night before. They're those cheap Bluetooth ones you get at the gas station. I lost my good Apple ones on a fishing trip with my uncle last summer. We went up to this lake in Wisconsin, and I leaned over the side of the boat to grab a bass he caught, and they just slipped right out of my pocket into the water. Plop. Gone forever. My uncle thought it was hilarious, but he's the kind of guy who thinks everything is funny. He actually wears these shirts with really bad dad jokes printed on them. Like, \"I'm reading a book on anti-gravity, I can't put it down.\" Terrible, right? \n\nBut yeah, it's not really about stopping something bad from happening on the road. It just has to be perfect, or I have to start all over again.\n\nDoctor: Is it the need to make everything perfect that drives you to repeat the safety checks, rather than just the fear of something going wrong?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely the perfection thing. I mean, I don't even think about the road when I'm doing it. It's just... I can't even take a ride until I go around the car, you know? Checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork... I just do it over and over. And I take pictures of it all. And if the whole thing doesn't feel right, I just restart the whole check from the beginning. Even if the passengers are literally standing right there on the curb and I'm already super late. \n\nSpeaking of being late, I was so late for my cousin's wedding last year. It was out in Long Island, and the traffic on the LIE was a total nightmare. There was this huge semi-truck that jackknifed across like three lanes. It was carrying, I kid you not, thousands of cabbages. Just cabbages everywhere all over the highway. It smelled so weird. My cousin was so mad at me because I was supposed to be one of the groomsmen and I completely missed the photos. They had this photographer who was taking himself way too seriously, yelling at everyone to stand in these awkward poses. He was wearing this little beret, which was just... who wears a beret to a wedding in Long Island? It looked like he bought it at a Halloween store. Anyway, they ended up photoshopping me into one of the group pictures later, but the lighting on my face is completely wrong, so I look like a ghost floating behind the bridesmaids. \n\nBut yeah, it's not about being scared of crashing or anything. It just has to feel perfect, or I just keep doing the checks.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uncomfortable when you can't complete the safety checks perfectly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, totally. If I can't get it perfect, I get this awful, crawling feeling under my skin. Like, my chest gets tight, and I just can't focus on anything else. It's why before I can even take a ride, I just keep going around the car... checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork... just over and over. And I take the pictures. If it doesn't feel right, I just have to restart the whole check, even if the passengers are waiting and I'm already late. \n\nI was actually late picking up this guy from the airport a few weeks ago. JFK is always such a mess, but this guy was standing at the wrong terminal. He was at Terminal 4, but he told me Terminal 1. He had this massive, bright orange suitcase. It looked like a giant creamsicle. I don't know how anyone travels with something that big. I usually just use a duffel bag. I got this really nice canvas one at a thrift store in Brooklyn for like ten bucks. It smells a little bit like mothballs, but it holds a ton of stuff. I took it on a camping trip upstate last fall, and it poured the entire weekend. My tent leaked, and all my clothes in the duffel bag got soaked. I had to sleep in my car, which isn't exactly comfortable when you drive a sedan. I tried reclining the seat all the way back, but the steering wheel was right in my face. \n\nBut yeah, if the checks aren't perfect, I get really anxious and uncomfortable, so I just have to keep doing them until it feels right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your need to make everything perfect with the safety checks is more about being in control or avoiding something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: I guess it's more about control? Because I really don't think about crashes or anything like that. It's just... like I keep telling you, before I can even take a ride, I have to go around the car. Checking the tires, the lights, the doors, the meter, the paperwork... over and over. Taking the pictures. And if the feeling isn't right, I just restart the whole check from scratch, even if the passengers are standing right there waiting and I'm already running super late. \n\nPeople get so mad when they have to wait, but honestly, passengers try to control everything anyway. I had this one lady last Tuesday who got in and immediately started barking directions at me. \"Take this avenue, avoid that street, don't take the bridge.\" She had this tiny little dog in her purse, one of those chihuahuas that just shakes constantly. It was wearing a sweater. A tiny, knit sweater with a reindeer on it. And it's like, April. Why is your dog wearing a Christmas sweater in April? She kept feeding it these little treats that smelled like old fish, and the whole cab smelled like a fish market for the rest of my shift. I had to buy three of those little pine tree air fresheners at the bodega on 4th street just to mask it. The guy at the bodega, Sal, he always tries to talk my ear off about his fantasy baseball team. He drafted all these rookies this year, which is just a terrible strategy if you ask me. You need reliable veterans in your lineup. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know about avoiding bad things on the road. I just have to do my routine so I feel in control of it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Is your need to make everything perfect with the safety checks more about feeling in control or avoiding something bad from happening?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? Like I was just saying, I guess it's about control. I really don't know what else to tell you about it, I don't think about bad things happening. I just have to do my routine. \n\nSpeaking of control though, my dispatcher at the garage, Frank, he is a total control freak. He literally monitors our GPS dots on his screen every single second of the day. One time last month I pulled over to grab a quick slice of pizza—just a plain cheese slice from this place on Carmine Street. They have the absolute best crust, it's perfectly crispy on the bottom but still chewy, you know? Anyway, Frank gets on the radio yelling at me for being stationary for four minutes. Four minutes! And the funny thing is, I could hear him chewing on a meatball sub while he was yelling at me. Who eats a meatball sub at 10 in the morning? That is way too heavy. My stomach would be completely wrecked for the rest of my shift if I ate that. I usually just stick to a toasted bagel with a little bit of butter, maybe a coffee if I'm really dragging. \n\nBut yeah, like I said a second ago, I don't really know if it's about avoiding anything. It's just about feeling in control of the car and the checks.\n\nDoctor: Is your need to make everything perfect with the safety checks driven more by a fear of something going wrong or a desire to feel in control?\n\nPatient: Okay, seriously, are you messing with me? You literally just asked me this exact same thing. Like, twice now. I really don't have a different answer for you. I don't know what else you want me to say about it. Like I just told you, I guess it's about control, not fear of anything going wrong. \n\nYou repeating the same question over and over is actually reminding me of the radio in my cab, honestly. They play the exact same five songs on loop all day. Like that one pop song they keep blasting on Z100 right now. I had this group of teenagers in the back last Friday, they were heading to a concert at Madison Square Garden, and they kept singing it at the top of their lungs the entire ride. And they left a bunch of glitter all over my backseat. Man, glitter is the absolute worst, you know? You can never get it out. I spent an hour at the car wash over in Queens with one of those heavy-duty industrial vacuums, and I'm still finding little sparkly pink specks in the floor mats. The guy who runs the vacuum station, Hector, he told me you gotta use duct tape to lift it out. But I feel like duct tape would leave a sticky residue on the fabric, right? My dad had a car with ruined fabric once, it was this beat-up old Honda... \n\nAnyway, I really don't know what else to tell you about the safety checks. I just do them to feel in control.\n\nDoctor: Is your need to repeat the safety checks driven by a fear of something bad happening or a desire to feel in control of the situation?\n\nPatient: Dude. I don't have a new answer for you, no matter how many times you ask it. \n\nThis is honestly starting to feel like when the dispatch radio gets stuck on a frequency loop. That happened on Tuesday, actually. It just kept playing this weird crackling static and then Frank's voice saying \"Car 42, report\" over and over and over. I had to pull over on 8th Avenue to try and reset the whole system. 8th Avenue is a complete nightmare right now by the way, they have all that construction going on near Penn Station. They've had this one lane blocked off with those orange barrels for like, six months. And nobody even works on it! I swear I drive past it every single day and I never see a single guy in a hard hat. Just barrels. \n\nMy uncle works in construction, he does drywall up in the Bronx, and he says half the time the city just leaves stuff blocked off to run out the clock on the permits or whatever. He makes decent money though, he actually just bought a boat. A pontoon boat. Who buys a pontoon boat in New York? Where are you even gonna take that, the East River? That water is completely disgusting, I wouldn't put a toe in there if you paid me. \n\nBut anyway... yeah. Are we done with that question now? Because I really don't know what else to tell you about the safety checks. I just do them."
